ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u13	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, Minister! The population of the elderly is growing in Poland. The need for 24/7 care services will increase. Demographic projections indicate a systematic increase in the population aged 60 and over. In 2050, people aged 60 and more living in cities will account for 23.5% the general population of the country, while the villagers of this age - 16.8%. From 2026 onwards, a dynamic increase in the number of people aged 80 and over will begin. In addition, there is an increase in the life expectancy of the population, which is uneven with regard to women and men - women live longer - which in turn will result in an increase in the number of seniors living alone. With life expectancy, there is an increased risk of limiting the ability to perform daily activities, thus increasing the risk of independence. The isolation and loneliness of older people can speed up this risk. In Poland, the number of private social services homes and in particular 24-hour care facilities is growing. Sadly, some of them had many irregularities and violations of the rights of those living there. Therefore, the State should ensure that the safety of the most vulnerable, needing support, 24/7 care and decent living conditions in such facilities are already ensured today. I would therefore like to ask: What changes are planned to be made to the operation of social assistance homes and 24-hour care facilities for persons with disabilities, chronically ill or elderly?
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u14	Thank you very much. Minister, please.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u15	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In view of demographic forecasts regarding the situation of our population and the change in the structure of the population, i.e. the increase in the number of older people, we want to ensure security and proper care both in social assistance homes and around the clock. Therefore, the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy has prepared an amendment to both the Social Assistance Act and the Mental Health Act. These proposals have now gone to the inter-ministerial consultation stage. These changes aim primarily, first, to improve the safety and conditions of stay of elderly and disabled persons in legally operating facilities providing 24-hour care, second, to reduce the scale of irregularities in 24-hour care facilities and to improve the effectiveness of sanctions imposed on operators without authorisation and thirdly, to facilitate establishments, especially those carried out to a small extent, to obtain authorisation by relaxing certain standards. I will move on to the issue of improving the safety and living conditions of the elderly and the disabled, who are staying in legally - I would like to stress - legally operating facilities that provide 24/7 care. The Ministry has prepared rules governing the admission and residence of persons with mental disorders and the leaving of 24-hour care facilities by such persons, which are similar to the existing arrangements for social assistance homes, providing for the reliance of admission to such facilities only after the court has granted the authorisation and after they have undergone periodic mental health tests to justify their further stay in the institution. In addition, rules for the application of direct coercion in institutions have been laid down by reference to the rules governing the application of direct coercion also in social assistance homes. These proposed provisions provide for the supervision of courts 24/7 on the legality of the admission and residence of persons with mental disorders, the observance of their rights and the control of the conditions under which they reside by reference to the Act on the Protection of Mental Health contained in the draft. In order to improve the conditions of stay, it is designed to reduce the number of places in 24-hour care facilities to 100, as well as to introduce qualification requirements for care providers and the employment rate of staff in these facilities, as the lack of such requirements at present makes care for residents of 24-hour care facilities often run by random persons, without basic knowledge in this area. In addition, the proposed provisions provide for a ban on the admission of minors to 24-hour care facilities, and such cases have also occurred in Poland, given the special protection of children's rights resulting from the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. It is the responsibility of the State to provide care for children without it and for this purpose institutions have been set up, such as foster care facilities or social assistance homes for children and young people with intellectual disabilities. Second thought. In terms of reducing the scale of irregularities in institutions that provide 24-hour care and improving the effectiveness of sanctions imposed on entities operating institutions without authorisation, they were legally operating there, and here we are talking about those illegally operating - the draft law contains provisions on extending the scope of control powers of inspectors of voivodship offices. It is expected that these operators will be more responsible without authorisation than one institution by increasing the amount of penalties imposed from 10 000. Or 20,000. Up to 40,000. In addition, it is designed to limit the possibility of obtaining authorisation to operate such an establishment by entities which the voivode has imposed in the past at least twice the penalty for running an establishment without authorisation or to suspend the operation of such an establishment. In addition, the draft amendment will allow the voivodeship to withdraw the permit to operate the facility at the same time if it is found to be life-threatening or health-threatening, and to order the institution to cease its operation with the decision of the rigour of immediate feasibility. This may be the case if a risk of life or health is found to be present in the facility, even if the facility is authorised. Until now, the possibility of ordering the activity to cease was for illegal establishments. We're changing that today, too. Thus, the governor had to first issue a decision withdrawing the permit, and only after it was legalized could he order the illegal activity of that institution to be suspended. All these changes are designed to improve safety and provide adequate care for older people, but also to ensure a sense of security for families who direct them to these facilities. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u32	Thank you very much, sir. I'm asking the Minister to answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u33	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, As regards domestic education, in 2016, the allocation algorithm introduced changes in funding for pupils who perform a school obligation or study outside the school in the so-called. home education. The amount of the subsidy for such a pupil from 2016 amounts to approximately 0,6 of the amount of the subsidy charged to other pupils. The reasonableness of this change is due to the much lower cost of teaching pupils who fulfil their schooling obligations outside school. Schools bear only small costs related to their classification and costs related to the provision of additional activities. Therefore, there is no justification for financing pupils who fulfil their school obligation outside the school of the amount of the subsidy allocated to pupils in school. The funds charged to pupils in home education go to local governments. The amount of funds transferred to schools for this purpose in the case of local schools depends on the decision of the local government, while in the case of subsidised schools it is determined in accordance with the provisions of the Education Act. The distribution of educational subsidies is largely made in connection with the costs of carrying out educational tasks, which is why educational subsidies charged to pupils in home education are intended to cover the educational costs of these pupils incurred by schools. There has never been such an assumption that the measures in question should be passed on to parents either directly or indirectly. The primary function of the education part of the general subsidy is the financial support of local self-government units in order to shape their income at a level appropriate to the dimension of educational tasks. The Law of 14 December 2016 amended the Education Law on the fulfilment of the school or study obligation outside the school in the so-called. home education. The changes entered into force on 1 September 2017 and consist of: the authorisation of such a form of education only by a director in the territory of the voivodship where the child resides, the need to have a public opinion on psychological and pedagogical counseling, the extension of the scope of support for children in domestic education by indicating the right of children to benefit from teaching assistance for the implementation of the curriculum base in school resources and the right to consultation to prepare for annual grading examinations. These changes aim to improve the conditions for training children in domestic education. An analysis of the level of funding for pupils in home education will be carried out once the above entry into force. changes in the implementation of the school obligation, the obligation to study outside the school. The Ministry of National Education will monitor this situation. As regards 2018, the method and level of funding for pupils in home education will remain the same as in the current year. In addition, the Law on the financing of educational tasks states that as from 2018 the grant from the budget of the local government unit is not payable to pupils carrying out annual pre-school preparation outside the pre-school institution. This solution is due to the fact that, unlike schools, in the case of kindergartens, the educational law does not provide for the tasks of kindergarten for a home-educated pupil. I just want to remind you that art. 35 Education law speaks of school duty, another, art. 36, clarifies this obligation, while education itself, so-called. Domestic education is defined in Article. 37 Educational Laws. And in the case of a student who does his duty outside the school, it is clear that what he has to do is a question of grading exams. For a number of months, a team composed of representatives of the Minister of National Education, parents and schools for which domestic education is taking place has carried out very detailed calculations of the costs we are talking about. For a reminder, earlier this amount was 1.0, which is just like the student in the institution where the classes are held, the class work in which we have teachers conducting these classes every day. As far as this change is concerned, it was due to the need to look at the cost of home education. As far as the work of this team is concerned, a great deal of time has been spent on how much the classification exam actually costs, how much time at which level, at which educational stage should be spent on such an examination. As far as the Ministry of National Education is concerned, the demands of representatives of domestic education have been taken into account, in other words, an extended catalogue of those rights which were granted to such a student. In other words, it is an opportunity to consult before classification exams and also to benefit from aid for the implementation of the programme base. On the other hand, all these decisions we are talking about have been consulted. We can say that local government units do not share the view that this amount is 0.6. [[♪ The bell ♪]] They think it's much smaller.

I assure you that the procedure in question is fully well founded. [[Sure, of course.]] The basis for initiating this procedure is the notification of 25 May 2017 submitted by a representative of the Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union of Policemen of the Lower Silesian Voivodeship of the suspicion of committing an act pursuant to Article. § 241 § 1 of the Penal Code, which consists in the public dissemination on 22 May 2017 by a journalist in the programme of Supervisiers of materials contained in the preparatory proceedings conducted by the District Public Prosecutor's Office in Poznań before these materials were disclosed in legal proceedings. This notification was sent through the Preparatory Procedure Department of the National Public Prosecutor's Office to the Regional Prosecutor's Office on 7 July 2017 in accordance with the jurisdiction. The proceedings were registered in the initial phase of its operation at the Warsaw-Mokotów District Public Prosecutor's Office in Warsaw, and subsequently after its initiation on 11 September 2017, following the creation of a new unit in Ursynów, they were transferred according to their properties to this unit. On 24 October, the prosecutor made a request for exemption by the court of the journalist of the TVN station from journalistic secrecy and sent it to the District Court for Warsaw-Mokotów in Warsaw for the dissemination of information from the preparatory proceedings conducted by the District Public Prosecutor's Office in Poznań. By order of 31 October 2017, following the examination of the prosecutor's application, the District Court for Warsaw-Mokotova pursuant to Article. 180 § 2 The Code of Criminal Procedure exempts a journalist from the obligation of journalistic secrecy, allowing him to be heard for the circumstances indicated in the prosecutor's request. Dear Izbo! On 17 November 2017, the National Public Prosecutor's Office examined the file of the present case and found that the prosecutor had requested that the journalist be released from secrecy very prematurely, that the prosecutor had not used any other means of evidence to determine the circumstances in which the prosecutor's disposition of the Article was required in the proceedings before the Court of Justice. 180 § 2 Code of Criminal Procedure. Only if the circumstances cannot be determined on the basis of other evidence, the Code of Criminal Procedure allows the possibility of obtaining evidence from a journalist's hearing, of course after his release by the court from secrecy. All observations resulting from the examination of the file of the case were submitted by letter of 17 November 2017 to the District Attorney in Warsaw for the purpose of the use of those comments, indicating, moreover, the need for the prosecutor to perform key activities in person. It was also pointed out that, in the event of an appeal against the decision of the court, it was appropriate for the prosecutor to make a complaint lodged by the journalist in view of the prematureness of the decision to repeal the secret and not to specify the circumstances to which the decision to exempt from secrecy should extend, of course, with emphasis on the journalist's right to conduct a journalist's investigation and publish its results. Dear Izbo! I assure you that this preparatory procedure is in no way aimed at gathering the evidence to show the journalist's responsibility and discredit the material issued in the programme. However, I assure you that the system cannot be run on such a scale without error, because the amount of interaction between the various integrated systems, the level of data combinations, the stages, the cycle of use of the document, the business process are so large that it is simply not possible to simulate all these combinations in test conditions. This concerns both state systems and commercial systems. Our tests with users prior to system startup, after internal tests, integration tests, safety performance tests, were carried out on data from several municipalities and with representatives of a number of ancients. Within the framework of these four iteration tests just with the ancients, after they were completed, at the time of the decision to implement, there were no identified blocking or critical errors. Due to the architecture of the system, how it is now built, due to the fact that there are local applications and a central base, and because of the lack of legal legitimacy, we could not decide to launch it by piloting, so it was required to implement in all ancients simultaneously. Currently we can actually talk about a period of system stabilization, which means increased readiness of implementation teams, both on the side of the central base provider and on the side of the local application provider. Extensive identification, analysis and correction of errors are still underway. The most critical problems of the first week have been corrected at this point, while the next ones are prioritised, and this is priorityd not only by us, but also in agreement and in contact with the ancients, in order to first improve what is most severe for them. At this point, customer service may still be extended, there may still be individual cases that cannot be handled by a given customer, and this is due to the fact that officials are still getting used to the system. Some errors at this point still require additional activities, so the service time of a single customer may be longer than some time ago. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u75	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for that question. Indeed, as part of many projects aimed at rebuilding the Polish Armed Forces' personnel reserves, the Ministry has launched a pilot program for voluntary training of students It is a programme implemented in agreement with the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. It assumes that state universities can apply for access, of course, to this programme, and then, also voluntarily, students at these universities can apply for military training: first theoretical training and then practical training of the field, after which they take an oath and become reserve soldiers. This programme is based on an agreement between the Ministry of National Defence and the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. Of the 98 state and military universities that are covered by this programme, 60 universities have volunteered to participate in this project. Considering the short part, ok. The three-week time when universities could apply and adopt appropriate senate resolutions, we believe that this is a good start, because it is almost 2/3 of institutions that are entitled to participate in the program. At these 60 universities, the creation of groups of students was started, which are expected to hold 30 hours of theoretical training. At this moment more than 130 groups were created, in which there are more than 4,000. students. This is, like I said, the beginning, the first semester. We assume that in the second semester there will be at least the same number of students and that the same groups will be created, so we assume that about 10,000 can apply to the program this year. students. As I said, theoretical training covers 30 hours of theoretical classes organized by universities, in university walls. The students receive basic knowledge of basic military training. Then those who pass will get a credit after these 30 hours and apply for access, willingness, are directed towards field training. To this end, they must pass through a sieve of military administration, the military command of additions. After qualifying, they go to a three-week field camp. 12 military units have been selected across the country where such training will be conducted. After these 3 weeks of intensive military training, they take a military oath, receive a mobilisation assignment and become reserve soldiers. For those people who are more ambitious and would like to undergo sub-office training, it is possible to extend intensive training by another 3 weeks and after the next 3 weeks and the sentence of the relevant exam, they become petty officers of the reserve. Training in the theoretical part is financed by the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. It is a cost of about PLN 1.5 million. The creation of this 10,000. training places during academic holidays, i.e. July, August and September, were defined as the cost in the budget of over PLN 200 million fully covered by the Ministry of National Defence. The training, as I said, is intense and provides for 12 hours of daily training activities, i.e. 12 times 45 minutes. Sunday's free. There is a very intensive training for shooting, and each participant will have the opportunity to learn about the general military knowledge, the basics of tactics, the basics of medicine of battlefields, field studies, topography, etc. We believe that this program has a chance to develop very well. Its strength is, you can say, this very voluntaryness. Those who have made themselves known - so far more than 4 000. Students and students - for this program, came in practically without any advertising, so we assume that in subsequent editions, i.e. In the summer semester, in which also students and universities will apply for this program, and then in the next editions this program will have a lot of interest. After piloting, since 2018, we plan to distribute this training into three modules, for the interested, of course. In the first year it will be a module for privates, the second year of training will be training for petty officers, and the third year will be training for reserve officers. It is worth noting that students and students will have the opportunity to undergo any military training for the first time in 9 years, because for the last 9 years there have been no such opportunities. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u83	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! So one at a time, the answers to these questions. The first question, about the deadline for piloting mental health centers. We are currently planning to pilot mental health centers, whose main objective is to move and test the new model, i.e. care under environmental conditions versus the current model of care in large psychiatric hospitals, namely stationary hospital care, began on 1 July 2018. This is the period required to prepare the conditions of this piloting well. We're in the final phase. That's the first reason. The second reason is that psychiatric care contracts are annexed by the end of June next year, so that the date of 1 July will be the date which will separate the units that will start piloting and will conclude such contracts. The second group are individuals who will remain in the current model of care and will proceed to competition proceedings from 1 July, i.e. in the first half of the year. This deadline is therefore 1 July 2018. We plan to have the pilot run for three years, 36 months. After each year of this programme, an evaluation will be prepared of the results that will flow from the pilot centres to the extent that these organisational and financial arrangements are sufficient or sufficiently motivated to provide such environmental care. This evaluation will also aim to modify the piloting process so that, after each year, this programme can be amended in the case of comments submitted. As regards the criteria for studios wishing to enter pilotage, these criteria were transmitted on 14 July 2017 when entities signed a declaration of accession to the pilotage of environmental protection. The criteria were developed by a team which was established in April ub. in the Ministry of Health, at the Ministry of Health, and a team of experts for the implementation of this pilot programme under the National Mental Health Programme. There is a very wide composition of experts, i.e. representatives of the Polish Psychiatric Society, the Association of Psychiatric Branches of General Hospitals and the Institute of Psychiatric and Neurology in Warsaw, as well as a national psychiatric consultant, a national psychiatric consultant or a very wide group of experts who were very responsible in establishing criteria. The specific criteria set out in the Annex to this declaration assume that the role of emergency care may be fulfilled by the 24-hour ER and need not be separated from the 24-hour ER in the psychiatric ward, only it may be a general ER operating in the hospital, where there will be a psychiatric care centre, which will be on 24-hour duty, so that patients who apply for emergency care will have a place where they will be able to report on 24-hour basis. Another criterion is to have at least three forms of care for at least two years out of all four, i.e. outpatient, environmental, day and 24 hours. So you have to have three of these four and a contract for the last two years. In addition, outpatient, environmental and day care must be organised in the area of operation of this centre outside this area, allowing only hospital care to be located. These criteria will not be modified during the selection of healthcare providers. Today we can say that as many as 139 actors have declared accession, which is a very good response from the environment. As far as further questions are concerned, so the actions that will be taken with regard to facilities that will not enter this pilotage, nothing changes here. As I said, the individuals who declared this will receive contracts under the rules set out in this pilotage, while those who have not decided to join it, in the first half of next year will proceed to normal competition proceedings on the same terms. so far, that is to say, the continuation of the agreements that are now in operation. As regards the fourth question, the date of the increase in funds for psychiatric care in the light of planned organisational changes, as regards the financing of all psychiatric care, it should be pointed out that despite the continued increase in the amount of expenditure on the provision of psychiatric care, the treatment of addiction in 2015 was 18.5%. in relation to 2011, i.e. for 4 years - 18%. The Ministry of Health, in line with the new edition of the National Mental Health Programme, generally aims to increase expenditure on all health sectors, including psychiatric care. As far as the valuation is concerned, this valuation was carried out at the Medical Technology Assessment Agency in the first half of the day. r. and yet due to the parallel ongoing work on piloting [[♪ The bell ♪]] and the financing rules for this pilotage have not entered into force. But this is a process that we're going to be working on in parallel now. It is about the agreements and rules for financing pilotage and, at the same time, the increase in expenditure for classic branches. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u139	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Podkarpackie local authorities asked me to make a request for the Ministry to examine the provisions of the article. Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 7 and 8 Waste Law prohibiting the treatment and import into the region of municipal waste management generated outside the region and the provisions of Article. 6d paragraph. 1 Law on the maintenance of cleanliness and order in municipalities ordering the organisation of public procurement procedures for the management of municipal waste. This is primarily due to the concern for the proper functioning of the municipal waste management system, which is financed by the fees of local authorities. The above-mentioned legislation leads to a situation in which, where there is one municipal waste treatment facility in the waste management region, it is a monopoly with all the negative consequences of the rates for the management of municipal waste, and where there are two, it creates ideal conditions for the creation of the so-called. price collation, which also adversely affects the level of the rates. Often, a municipality at the border of a region which has a closer facility for waste treatment in another region is doomed to the prices of installations from its region proposed in a quasi-professional procedure which, in the event of the existence of one or two economic operators, contradicts market rules. At the beginning, I will try to refer to the words of the Member who focused her attention on a law which is not the responsibility of the Minister of the Environment, namely the Economic Freedom Act. In the area of collective responsibility, Minister Warchoł organised a very good conference, and I know that he is preparing legislation that will help fight to ensure that the collective responsibility of such entities is enforced. We, on the basis of those records that are already there, did not take any action because they do not function, we need to increase this area of responsibility. Questions about what is in the authority of the Minister of the Environment are the easiest to answer, but I feel this responsibility in those areas that you mentioned. I would like to say that we work very closely with ministers, not only on the occasion of the Demoted Land Team, but also on other occasions. The first thing we've done, what every person who's responsible and managing a department of the economy is taking responsibility... It was crucial to identify the biggest problems in waste management. This is the balance we have done and consistently we are doing it. The first issue is the question of competence, the way in which the responsibility for waste management is carried out; it is largely on the part of local authorities. And very different, depending on local governments, it looks like, and Stalowa Wola is an example that you can. But we're in Warsaw and let's see, really only 4% what is sorted or divided, because it is not divided into material fractions, it is suitable for recycling. These are not data from the Ministry of the Environment, but data from Europe. This responsibility of local governments is very important, which is why, seeing this need, we will not only be as we have done... One of the first legislative actions we took was to strengthen the in-house mechanism so that local governments could commission their entities without a tender. This is a very important tool, which was lacking in this amendment, which introduced the so-called. A trash revolution. I'm against any revolution at all. Last night, about an hour. 2 I witnessed a debate on the Bolshevik Revolution. In general, law, legal order likes certain evolutions, especially in the area of waste management. We have implemented actions resulting from ex ante conditionality, so we have introduced a regulation on selective collection of waste, in Poland as a whole it is harmonised, which will give us that we are implementing, and here I refer to what is the largest important locomotive in this area, i.e. the economy of closed circulation. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u192	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Dariusz Piontkowski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u193	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! This law, as we discussed in the Environment Committee, concerns two elements. The first element is the implementation of EU law, several directives, which concern in particular hazardous waste, their transport, possibly the elimination of such waste, the hazardous waste clause, and then the road that these waste should go through. Since these directives should be implemented quite quickly, the draft law therefore provides for a 14-day vacathio legis so that these elements of the law enter into force as soon as possible so that we do not have problems with the institutions of the European Union. The second part of this law concerns this sealing of the waste management system, which I was just talking about. This is mainly about the database on products and packaging and waste management, i.e. the so-called. BDO. This base is to be built at the beginning of next year and is to be gradually expanded by further elements. This law will give the possibility to extend this base to include additional modules so that it covers a maximum range of information on waste. It will allow the waste to be recorded at virtually every stage of its existence - from the moment the waste manufacturer transmits it when the transport company receives it, transports it and transmits it to further processing. This electronic system will provide an opportunity to control how companies carry out their duties. You will be able to check the discrepancies between the declared activities of waste companies and what is the reality. Because it will happen almost in real time, because waste companies, when transporting waste, will have to have generated information in this waste transfer card at the start of transport, so that it is known at which point they are, then the receiving company will also have to confirm the collection of these waste, indicate their mass, will of course be possible corrections, because, for example, I do not know, excessive drought or rain or snow can cause an increase in this mass, and this will be possible to correct. But just the fact that two companies that work together in the treatment and transport of waste will have to confirm the same data will make it difficult to introduce fictional data. Fictitious data and fictitious waste trading were largely the cause of these negative phenomena which the Minister had just mentioned, which we discussed in this Chamber. They are the ones that are largely generating the so-called formation of the so-called. wild landfills, while at the same time being a form of unfair competition against those companies that have made large investments in waste sorting plants, waste treatment plants and react legally. This waste database will be administered in accordance with the provisions of the Act by the General Environmental Inspector. It was thought that this would be a chance to merge this data across the country, because there are also companies that operate not only in one voivodship or one city, but in several voivodships, and this was partly out of control by the marshals. Therefore, the additional responsibilities of the Environmental Inspection will increase, and therefore the bill provides for a slightly higher revenue from the registration fee, an annual fee, which has so far been split 50-50 between the Ministry and the State budget, so that these funds are more likely to be inspected. In addition, one person will be employed in each voivodship inspector, who will only supervise and control this waste base. We hope, because here I am talking about both the government and the Members, and I am thinking of all those who are interested in the state of environmental protection, that these electronic data collected in the waste database will enable real control of what is happening with the waste, will allow cross-checks and detection of these irregularities, which we talked about a few minutes ago, and also eliminate from the market at least parts of companies that behave unfairly in this market. Hence, the Law and Justice Club, of course, requests that this law be adopted and that it be implemented as soon as possible. These elements, the rules on the waste database are only to enter into force in accordance with the Act from January 2020, as we realise that it is time for companies to adapt to this. [[♪ The bell ♪]] and to enter these waste databases. The club also proposes some amendments, mainly legislative ones, but also about what will happen to the voivodship's waste data. We'll take it as historical data. [[Applause]]

The bill is now moving towards a rational use of existing mechanisms. The proposed rules enable municipalities to better implement the Law on sobriety and combating alcoholism. It is recommended that all alcoholic beverages, including beer and drinks with an alcohol content of up to 4.5% , have been included in the limit which specifies the maximum number of authorisations, while keeping up to date the principle that the limit is set separately for points of sale of beverages intended for consumption outside the place of sale and at the place of sale. The project gives the local authorities the right to prohibit the sale of alcohol between 22 and 6 a.m. and allows them to set restrictions for their respective parts. The idea is to limit sales where excessive consumption poses the biggest problem, among others. in the centers of big cities and resorts. However, this solution can be applied flexibly. Hourly forks within the nighttime period can be shaped by local government at its own discretion - different solutions are possible in specific support units throughout the municipality. In addition, the project introduces a ban on drinking alcohol in public places, while at the same time giving the local government the opportunity to freely shape exemptions. The Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, following its first reading, met at its meeting on 21 November 2017, taking into account the positive opinion of the Committee on Health, held a discussion and considered the draft law presented. The Commission rejected the amendments tabled, accepted the self amendment amending the title of the draft law resulting from a typographical error. The second proposal dealt with a different approach to identifying a public place. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u225	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present my position on the amendment of the law on sobriety education and combating alcoholism, print No 1988. The proposed act is the answer to the government's submissions... Local authorities, of course, have been asking for a long time to respond to the concentration of alcohol sales points in the centres of large cities, as well as in resorts. The purpose of the Act is to amend the provisions that raise doubts about interpretation and to regulate at the statutory level issues that enable municipalities to better achieve the objectives of the Law on Sobriety and Antitrust, i.e. restricting the availability of alcoholic beverages. The presence on one street of several or sometimes dozens of shops selling alcohol, and such events often occur in large cities, including coastal or mountain resorts, often 24/7, results in a significant increase in cases of disorder and order and more serious crime in the area. This is confirmed by the threat maps, especially drawn up by the police, recently very popular on the Internet. This in turn leads to gradual degradation of city centers and reduced attractiveness not only of city centers, but also of tourist attraction of individual resorts, especially those most visited. The proposed solutions give local authorities a more effective tool to respond to such situations. The current limit for alcohol sales points for the whole area of the city or the municipality will be able to be regionalised. The City Council will be able to apply a separate limit in the area where the problem arises. The city council will also be able, in justified cases, to restrict the sale of alcohol during the night. Such a tool gives the local authorities an important opportunity to put pressure on sellers to ensure peace and order around the point of sale of alcohol, especially in the night. It should be stressed that the bill is not a prohibition measure. In any of the paragraphs, it does not introduce an arbitrary ban on the sale of alcohol, but merely gives local governments the opportunity to react in pathological situations, if such situations occur in a given municipality, because they cannot be generalized, such situations occur not in all municipalities, but in a large part. The decision on the possible use of these tools will be made by the Council each time knowing the specificities of local problems. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I am handing over two amendments to the Marshal's hands. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u238	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Members of Free and Solidarnia, I present a report on the draft law amending the law on sobriety education and combating alcoholism and some other laws. The draft law aims to amend the provisions that raise doubts about interpretation and to regulate these issues at the statutory level, which will enable the municipalities to better achieve the objectives of the Law on Sobriety Education and Against Alcoholism, i.e. restricting the availability of alcoholic beverages. Alcohol consumption in Poland is higher than average consumption in the European Union. Research shows that the consumption of alcoholic beverages in our country will continue to increase. This situation is very worrying and I therefore fully support the initiative taken by a group of Members who want to introduce new tools into the bill that local authorities will be able to use. Many diseases caused by abuse of alcoholic beverages, e.g. injuries, cancers, cardiovascular diseases and many other diseases directly or indirectly contribute to death. In Poland, almost 10,000 people die from alcohol abuse in the course of the year. persons. Looking at such statistics, we need to take decisive action that will be effective and effective. To date, the Municipality Council has determined the number of points of sale of alcoholic beverages, not including beer or alcohol up to 4.5%. Alcohols such as beer are much cheaper and more accessible to the general public. Many studies show that it is the beer that is most common among young people. Universal availability and huge number of points of sale make it impossible to effectively control compliance with the provisions of the Law on sobriety and combating alcoholism, among others. in the context of compliance with the ban on the sale of alcohol to minors. Reducing physical and economic accessibility is a recognised worldwide mechanism contributing to reducing alcohol consumption. Let me remind you that the bill introduces e.g. new rules for determining the number of authorisations for the sale of alcohol by the municipality board. The Council will be able, by resolution, to set a maximum number of authorisations for individual support units separately and to introduce restrictions on the sale of alcoholic beverages intended for consumption outside the place of sale during the night. The contested law provides numerous tools for local authorities to reduce the sale of alcoholic beverages and indirectly reduce the consumption of alcohol by the local community. I believe that local governments will use the tools proposed by the law with caution and full responsibility. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u262	Thank you, Marshal. High Izbo! To answer questions, I would like to say, that's where I'm going to start, why this is a parliamentary project. That's why it's a parliamentary mode. I answer the question of whether there have been consultations with local authorities and how these environments relate to this. Well, in the case of the Polish City Association, when I was still a member of the board of directors of this board, it was one of the priorities to introduce such solutions. The Union of Polish Metropolises, the second largest local government organisation, also supports these solutions. Therefore, local government officials from many cities - I could exchange, from Torun, Rzeszów, Warsaw, Poznań, Przemyśl, Puławy and many, many cities - support these solutions. They were only impatient about why they had to wait so long for such tools. Ladies and gentlemen! I prepared this bill because even as a local governmentman, I was approached by residents who expected the cities to deal with the problem of the high density of alcohol sales points in the centers of large cities. The centres of cities such as Warsaw, Poznań, Wrocław, Toruń and Kraków are to be a showcase of these cities, not a shy gutter. The centre is to be attractive to tourists and residents, not to offer as the main attraction of the group sitting in front of points selling alcohol. And that's why this project. So that this is not a central solution, but a solution that takes into account the specifics of each city, we give tools to local governments that will react only if they notice that it is the right of the inhabitants, I stress: the right to feel safe in their neighborhood is being violated. No prohibition is introduced. No rights and freedoms are restricted in this project. Everything is decided by the authority that is closest to the people. And that's what these tools are for. The first such tool is to regionalise sales limits. The question was why the beer was covered by such a limit. I will ask another question: Why have all the other alcohols been subject to limits so far and not beer? I like beer very much, I don't mind connoisseurs of this liquor, but I don't really understand why this alcohol would be treated on some special basis. We want to treat him evenly. It's an hour limit. The question was, isn't it too restrictive? These are forks, maximum range. I would like to remind you that in the project submitted by the Civic Platform Group last year, local governments were given the opportunity to prohibit full-time sales. That was a restrictive restriction. We have found that these problems do not occur throughout the day, so the possibilities of introducing restrictions should be narrowed down only at hours. 22. If the government finds that problems arise after hours. 23, after 24 or another hour, then within these forks he will be able to flexibly decide in what hours introduces this restriction. I would also like to talk about the consultation process, because there were such allegations here that there was no consultation. Ladies and gentlemen, I have been consulting this project from the beginning. That was the first issue I dealt with here in the Sejm in the D.A. team. Polish cities. There have been talks with all stakeholders, residents, uniformed services, alcohol sellers, alcohol producers, health experts. There really was a lot of consultation. The proof that there were so many of them is, at least, the fact that there was not a single vote at the committee meeting the day before yesterday that there was no consultation or lack of time. We differed in opinions, but that was not the voice. I'll be finishing, of course, Mr. Marshal, in a moment. On the other hand, these consultations led to this compromise dimension of this law, in which I also took into account the voice which was voiced by Mr Wilczyński at the committee meeting. I promised at the committee meeting that I would reflect on whether the general ban on drinking in public places, excluding by the city council, and this exclusion, where there was indeed a lack of this annotation, who indicated the places to be consumed, was a legitimate solution. We have tabled a club amendment in which we follow this lead, with this intention not only suggested by Mr Wilczyński - he emphasized this at the committee's meeting, for which I thank him - but also representatives of other communities, so that the local government may possibly complement the list of places where alcohol is prohibited, but we do not introduce this general prohibition clause. The criteria that we introduce are clear to decide on concessions. They shouldn't raise doubts in my opinion. And one last thing. The second amendment, which we are making, is an amendment which also stems from such reflections - also from Mr Kukiz, because I also talked about it with Mr Józwiak - to introduce a solution that will facilitate the collection of concessions in a situation where the point does not respect the order and order of its area and does not inform the relevant uniform services. Until now, it's been a dead law because it was miswritten. In line with these intentions, we make such an amendment. In conclusion, I would like to say that I am fully convinced that the consultation was sufficient, that this act is compromise, that it takes into account the voice of many different environments - and dealing with the subject and political - and that is why I would like to ask you not to make this a good tool for the local governments of the subject for some political fight, but to support it as a pro-government tool. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u330	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, First of all, I would like to stress that the role of the State Election Commission is not to assess the reasonableness of the project presented by the applicants by the project promoters. The role of the State Election Commission is to evaluate the proposed provisions on the electoral code, because that is all I will focus on, in terms of their usefulness and guarantees, so that the next electoral process that awaits us, the local government elections, as well as the next, can be conducted in the manner that so far, i.e. efficient, fast, without endangering its course, without the threat of disturbing or even destabilizing its course. As far as speed issues are concerned, with transparency, we see in the draft a number of provisions that are undoubtedly positive. I'm going to start with, for example, the election card, which is a rough card, which is basically our consultation that we had with the voters, had a similar effect: the voters were mostly in favour of introducing a rough card. We also accept and very much welcome the changes that apply to, for example: trust husbands are being designed a whole new chapter on trust husbands with a significant extension of their powers, as well as the issue of cameras that will be installed in electoral premises, in particular if they are directed at a committee that counts votes. This also applies, of course, to the provision that causes the local government elections not to take place at the same time as the constitutional referendum proposed by your President. These are the pros we see, but the point is that these pros don't cloud our cons. Because there are unfortunately a lot of these negatives and we are looking at it, as I said, through the prism of guarantees that will give us the opportunity to conduct elections in a timely manner, as we have done so far in 2015. There are two key issues: the issue of electoral commissioners and the question of the National Election Office. These are two of our weapons, thanks to which the State Election Commission can act efficiently and can have the support of institutions that have been tested and with which it has been cooperating for years. The issue of electoral commissioners. It must be said that the projectors, by introducing electoral commissioners who are not judges, are clearly leaving the judicial system. Commissioners who have so far been appointed by the State Election Commission are judges who are designated as candidates by the Minister of Justice. This is the first sieve that candidates go through. The Minister then presents these nominations to us, but we, on the basis of the entire dossier we receive from the Minister, can also hold a meeting with the candidate on the basis of our knowledge of the judges' candidates, and after this meeting, after examining the documents, we are clear whether this is the person who will properly carry out the duties of the electoral commissioner. And what is worth emphasising is that judges, or the quality of independence, independence is in this case the characteristic that characterizes judges. For this reason, the legislator in 1990 considered judges to be those who would ensure honesty and objectivity in the election process. The projectors propose to move away from this system and introduce, instead of Commissioners of Judges, Commissioners whose only requirement will be to have a higher education. It will be 16 regional commissioners and 380 district commissioners whose National Election Commission will have to set up within 2 months within 60 days. I would like to stress that the appointment of 400 electoral commissioners within 2 months by the State Election Commission, which consists of only 9 people, is almost bordering on a miracle. I would like to draw attention to the fact that I am getting the views, which are also expressed by the Members of the Projectors, that this is not a problem. The Commission has 400 officials, a huge financial budget, and therefore there is no problem with recruiting these candidates. However, I would like to draw attention to a point, as the electoral code states that Commissioners are appointed by the State Election Commission and they are agents of the State Election Commission. How can we, as we are suggested, establish a specific outsourcing, headhunter companies that will recruit candidates? This is not recruitment of employees or managers to a company of commercial law, but it is recruitment of people who must have professional preparation, practical preparation, knowledge of the electoral code. Therefore, it is not possible to appoint or recruit candidates for election commissioners to other companies, even officials from the National Election Office, because they are, with all due respect, officials who are not qualified do not have the appropriate knowledge of electoral law to make such appointment. In this respect, only those who we trust and who we will be sure will carry out the duties of the electoral commissioners in a proper manner can be our agents. Another point I would like to mention is the Commissioners. Namely, new Commissioners are given new responsibilities, which will be transferred from the municipalities' councils to Commissioners in terms of the division of municipalities into districts and also districts into circuits. We here, as I said, are not dealing with substantive issues. Issues related to one or other substantive provisions for the State Election Commission are of little importance. We are interested in the issue of regulations that will allow for the smooth conduct of elections, but if you take away the obligations to divide municipalities into municipalities' councils, you have to remember That it wasn't the mayor who divided the communes into districts. The uncle's just making a request to the council. I think that the council, which consists of 15 and 45 councillors depending on the area of the commune and population, is somehow able to cope, but if this is the will of the projectors, then I just want to draw attention to the following. The current system of division of the municipality into districts is three-level, i.e. from the municipality, then there is a reference to the Commissioner and at the end - a reference to the State Election Commission. There's no further judicial appeal. The project shortens this three-level system to two-level: it will only be the decision of the Provincial Commissioner, after which it will appeal to the State Election Commission. For this reason, in the current state of law and in the legal form of the designers designed by you, there is no legal appeal to the court, and it must be borne in mind, and I am giving consideration to the applicants, and above all to the Members, that 1,5 years ago, the Constitutional Tribunal in the case of signage. Act P 5/14 stated that the failure to appeal against the decision of the State Election Commission, which ultimately confirms the decision on the division of the municipality into districts, is contrary to Article. 45 constitutions, that is, with the right to court, and with the article. 165, which guarantees the judicial protection of local government units. The Court has made it clear that the administrative court to which an appeal should be sought is competent. This is something that the designers, the designers, do not mention, and this is undoubtedly a very important issue for the municipalities and for the voters in the municipalities. If they feel victims of the division, they should have the right to appeal to the court in the end. And one more thing about the commissioners. Namely, it is designed to reduce the number of Commissioners from 51 to only 16, which will be contrary to the rights of committees wishing to submit a notification of registration... [[Voice from the room: No way.]] ...because, for example, There are six commissioners in the Mazowieckie voivodship, so even if a committee that was founded at the ends of the voivodship wants to register, it has a short way to the nearest commissioner, it is several kilometers, it does not have to go to Warsaw almost 200 km, and this threatens to reduce the number of commissioners. Only up to 1 in each voivodship. I am not saying that this, let's say, discourages, but that it will certainly be an element that hinders this communication and the possibility of registering, all the more so because the Provincial Commissioner has only 3 days to register the application, and remember that on average in each voivodship several hundred committees are reported. In the Mazowieckie voivodship there are an average of 1 000, therefore it is unimaginable that within 3 days the newly elected Provincial Commissioner, who has no experience in this respect, can register these committees at that time. And another case, which is important to us. The second pillar on which the State Election Commission bases its tasks, its activities, is the National Election Office. The Head of the National Election Office under the electoral code is the executive body of the State Election Commission. He is also Secretary of the State Election Commission with an advisory voice and is appointed by the State Election Commission at the request of the Chairman of the State Election Commission. It cannot be forgotten that on the shoulders, so to speak, the National Election Office has the entire burden of making choices in an administrative, technical, financial sense. The State Election Commission is only nine judges, with no staff and no budget. If projectors now want to change this system and propose to deprive the State Election Commission of free choice of its co-worker, because we are working with the National Election Office, otherwise it would be impossible to hold elections if they wanted to bind the hands of the State Election Commission and make it possible for the State Election Commission to select from among the three candidates submitted by the political authorities... So this political element is being transferred to a potential head of the National Election Office, which is not advisable when the State Election Commission is an apolitical body, and it would be good for the apolitical body to cooperate with the State Election Commission. This is a bit like in this case with the Ford car - you can choose any color, provided it is black. Here, too, project promoters are trying to limit or essentially deprive the State Election Commission of the free choice of a collaborator at the head of the office on which the elections are based in a technical sense. It is also important to bear in mind what is obvious that the newly appointed head of the National Election Office will certainly want to rely on employees to whom he trusts, i.e. new people, which will cause further perturbations related to the electoral process. ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u618	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! After such a comprehensive discussion about Poland's future in the area of the forthcoming elections, we are faced with something that certainly should not arouse the greatest political emotions, and is strictly addressed to all of us, because we are all consumers as we sit here. The subject matter is very mild, very delicate, very important for each of us every day, and concerns the powers which the petition committee believes should be granted to anyone who will benefit from the possibilities of the so-called. Hands. But what is it? Ladies and gentlemen! The author of the proposed law is Sebastian Adamowicz from Gdansk, who believes that the current legal situation resulting from the provisions of the Civil Code is insufficient to protect consumers. The law is very short because it contains an amendment to the article. 568 add Article. 568 with the following content: If the buyer is a consumer, the seller's liability for the warranty runs again from the day of the exchange of goods for fault-free goods. The contested draft law amending the Civil Code provides for a very important social impact, as I said, because it will lead to greater consumer protection. Consumers have the right to exchange goods for rights free of defects, but what is most important is the question of the date of liability of the seller in such a case, because it does not change and counts from the date of issue of the original purchased goods. This regulation is intended to ensure that, in the event of the exchange of warranties for goods free of defects, the seller's liability for the same warranty will run from the beginning from the date on which the goods are released free of defects. This provision will be comparable, identical to the guarantee liability regime, which will allow even greater consumer protection and, in some situations, ensure the undisputed possibility of exercising warranty rights. Ladies and gentlemen! Why can petitions serve the proposed changes? They may mobilise sellers to pay great attention to the quality of the goods sold and to cooperate with those producers who will be able to ensure their quality. For manufacturers, the law will be a trigger for effective response when consumers find faults in their products. This will be a measurable factor for honest manufacturers and sellers offering good quality goods and services. Another important social effect of the proposed law is a possible positive impact on the budgets of a small group of households. By increasing consumer protection, the consumer will have the right to exchange goods at a time when, under the current rules, he would already be forced to cover the costs of repairing goods or buying new goods or services. If we look at this from the point of view of economic operators, the proposed law may have a minor negative impact on their revenues or costs if they would be obliged to trade goods several times for fault-free at the request of the same consumer. However, we must first of all point out that complaint procedures are a source of information for a reliable producer, a signal from the market on improving the quality of the goods offered, and the complaint procedure, from which the consumer is satisfied, is a very important element of marketing policy for a large number of companies. This approach to business results in a measurable increase in revenue, which will compensate for possible minor negative effects on the company concerned. Ladies and gentlemen! This draft law was addressed by the Confederation of Leviathan and, in its opinion on the contested law, the Confederacy points out that the most important problem of this amendment is not the direction of change, but the lack of a comprehensive regulation to amend other provisions on warranties which would allow the trader to complete at some stage the process of multiple replacement of goods, for example by enabling the trader to withdraw from the contract and refund the paid claim to the consumer. It may occur when a product with unusual properties exhibits a certain defect after a short period of time, e.g. After a 6-month period of use, the consumer will be replaced cyclically as he will only be interested in the short-term use of the product. Ladies and gentlemen! Given that this draft, in the opinion of the committee, will have an absolute positive impact on the functioning of consumers in the economic market and on increasing their protection comparable to the statutory formula of guarantees and the same conditions regarding the term of protection, the Committee on Petitions will ask that this law be welcomed by a favourable eye... so that, thanks to State aid, it can, of course, go to the codification committee, so that it can be further processed in substance over these records, taking into account of the opinion of the parties who issued the opinions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u622	Dear Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on amendments to the Civil Code, in relation to the novel. As regards the change, this amendment concerns the modification of the strictest principle of liability of the seller for absolute liability. This is the responsibility for the effect itself. This amendment concerns one of the four rights that a buyer may exercise in the event of a physical defect or a legal defect. An examination of this very laconic provision, as the previous speakers said, could indicate that the extension of the period in principle in which the consumer can exercise his warranty rights would be of great benefit to him, because it is theoretically possible that the consumer exercising this right will be able to exercise several times the right to exchange things free of defects under his right. Theoretically, therefore, this may happen so that the duration of the seller's liability will extend whenever the buyer exercises his right, times twice. Theoretically, this time can be extended indefinitely. Let me remind you that today this right is limited by a 2-year binding term. Today everyone, both the consumer and the seller, knows that each of these four rights can be realized within a maximum period of 2 years, because we have a 2-year deadline for detecting defects. At the same time, I would like to point out that one can imagine a situation of this kind, that the buyer will exercise for several years the right to exchange things for free of defects and, finally, will benefit from his farthest-reaching right to withdraw from the contract. Thus, the amendment of this provision, which points to the benefits that the consumer will achieve, raises some doubts that what is really meant for the consumer to benefit this additional benefit may turn against the consumer. Please note, first of all, that the trader who is a seller, aware of the possible powers that the buyer will exercise, will do anything, and can do so in the light of current legal provisions, in order to prevent him from making use of the exchange of goods free of defects, and will never fulfil that claim. Please also note the second circumstance. If a market watcher concludes that such a risk of warranty liability will continue or may continue indefinitely, he must take this into account in the costs of his business. If he has to make things available several times and it's not known when it's going to end, then in his economic account, the event also has to be included. I think we should also consider this entrepreneur's risk, as it seems. In a situation where such consumer behaviour is widespread and the scale of this responsibility and costs is increasing, there may even be a financial failure of the entrepreneur. We were supposed to protect this consumer and this protection was supposed to last for a longer period of time, but because of the absence of this entrepreneur who, due to financial risks, would not actually benefit from these opportunities. I think that it is also important in the course of the discussion - because we are in favour of this bill being passed to the committee responsible - to eliminate this interruption of the term of exercise of the rights with the guarantees that we are dealing with in this amended provision. It seems appropriate to consider how to limit the rights of a consumer exercising his right to exchange things for fault-free goods and how these other rights would be exercised in the event that the consumer wishes to exercise his rights for guarantees. I therefore request that the project be submitted to the committee responsible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u632	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I will briefly present the draft position of the government towards the Commission's draft law amending the Civil Code Act. This is a project because the proposal prepared by the Ministry of Justice has been addressed to inter-ministerial arrangements and opinions. That deadline hasn't expired yet. The draft position stresses that, in order to protect the buyer's interest in the provision of defective goods in the performance of the sales contract, the institution's liability for the defect was introduced in the sales regulations, whereby the seller's responsibility was established in a manner independent of his fault, from the fault of the persons who act on his account, from the damage resulting from the provision of defective goods and from the seller's knowledge of the existence of the defect. According to Art. 568 § 1 first sentence of the Civil Code the seller shall be liable for the warranty if the physical defect is detected before the expiry of 2 years, and in the case of property defects - before the expiry of 5 years from the date of issue of the goods to the buyer. In view of the wording of this provision, it is appropriate to consider the statement set out in the explanatory memorandum of the proposal that de lege summers in case of exchange of things for free of defects under the warranty, the consumer may be deprived of protection. The protection of consumers as a weaker party to the obligation relationship requires the formulation of rules on guarantees for sale, which will ensure that they have the highest possible level of security of transactions. To this end, there is no doubt that the proposed solution extends the term of liability of the guarantors in the case of sales contracts concluded with the consumer. Therefore, the direction of the proposed changes deserves approval. The project shows that the seller's liability for the warranty runs again from the day of the exchange for free from defects. Therefore, as regards the proposed wording of the provision, it should be noted that the wording used from the date of the exchange may give rise to interpretation doubts as to the specific moment from which the seller's liability for the warranty should be calculated after the replacement of the defective goods. It cannot be excluded that, in practice, this wording will not be interpreted as the day of giving away things free from defects to the consumer, but for example. as the day the seller sends such a thing to the consumer. In order to remove any doubts in this respect, the proposed provision should be clarified by clearly indicating that it is the date of issue of the said, i.e. free of defects, to the consumer. I conclude that, taking into account the need to strengthen the protection of consumer rights as a weaker party to the commitment relationship, the draft law amending the Civil Code law should be aimed at further legislative work, subject to the comments made in the draft position of the government contained in the draft. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-11-23-sejm-52-2.u636	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present the club's position on the committee proposal for a law amending the Civil Code Act. The aim of the proposed project is to improve consumer protection. According to current legal regulations, when goods are exchanged for defective goods under the guarantee, the term of guarantee shall run again from the time when the goods are delivered free of defects. The situation is different when the consumer decided to exercise his right of exchange for the benefit of guarantees free of defects. In this case the warranty period shall not be extended and counted from the moment of purchase of the goods. This state of affairs does not change even a multiple exchange of acquired things. As a consequence, such rules, where more than 6 months have passed since the original goods were issued, the enforcement of their warranty rights may be significantly impeded or even impossible. According to the project submitted in case of replacement of goods for warranty-free goods, the term of liability of the seller for the warranty will run from the beginning from the date of issue of the product free of defects. In this way, the provisions on guarantees and guarantees will coincide. There is no doubt that in the market it is usually the consumer who is weaker and that the protection of his rights should be the primary focus. The use of warranties often involves the need to cease the use of the goods while processing the application. A person who, as a result of poor quality of the goods and evident fault of the seller, is forced to exchange the goods for guarantees several times, is de facto deprived of the possibility of using the goods purchased. In such a situation, non-extension of the term with guarantees is detrimental to the consumer and gives an unfair advantage to the seller. Therefore, the Club The Modern Parliament will support the bill submitted and vote in favour of referring it to further work in the committee.
